[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU),
module name `tpu`, implementing the EXACT per-cycle behavior of the logikbench
`tpu` benchmark (zeroasiccorp/logikbench, MIT).

FUNCTION: computes C = A * B tile products on an 8x8 grid of processing
elements. B (the "weight" matrix, 8-bit signed) is loaded into the array and
held; A (the "activation" matrix, 8-bit signed) streams through; result rows
C (32-bit signed) drain out the bottom.

PROTOCOL:
  1. Weight load: assert w_valid for 8 cycles; w_data carries one weight row
     per cycle, 8 signed bytes packed { col 7 ... col 0 }, BOTTOM matrix row
     first (cycle k carries B[7-k][*]; the weights shift south). After 8
     cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
  2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle (packed the
     same way). Row m of the result, C[m][*] packed { col 7 ... col 0 } as 8
     signed 32-bit words, appears on c_data with c_valid high exactly 15
     (= 2*8-1) compute cycles after its A row was accepted.
  - The array advances only while w_valid is low: asserting w_valid stalls
    any in-flight computation (and reloads weights). Zeros are injected while
    a_valid is low, so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
  - Multiple A tiles may stream back-to-back against the same loaded weights.

CYCLE ACCURACY: c_valid is compared against the original design EVERY cycle;
c_data is compared on every cycle where c_valid is high. The 15-cycle
compute-latency and the stall semantics are part of the contract.

Ports:
  clk, rst (async active-high reset, asserted only before the stream begins)
  w_valid, w_data[63:0]
  a_valid, a_data[63:0]
  c_valid, c_data[255:0]

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` is the
original logikbench RTL (tpu_pe / tpu_array / tpu: PE mesh, per-row input
skew, per-column output de-skew, valid pipeline). It passes every cycle of the golden
trace in `vectors.dat` (directed zero/identity/extreme tiles, random tiles,
back-to-back streams, a_valid gaps, mid-drain weight reloads, random soak).
Study it, then optimize while preserving the cycle-accurate behavior. The
sibling benchmark `tpu_spire` shares the identical testbench and vectors
with a SpireHDL starting point.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Processing element (PE) for the weight-stationary systolic array (TPU MXU).
//
// Each PE holds one stationary signed weight 'w'. Two independent datapaths
// share the cell:
//
//   Weight load (ld): the weights form a per-column shift register that loads
//   from the top. On each ld cycle the PE captures the weight from the PE above
//   (w_north) and presents its stored weight downward (w_south). After N load
//   cycles every PE in a column holds its final stationary weight.
//
//   Compute (en): the activation flows west to east (registered, one hop per
//   cycle) and the partial sum flows north to south, accumulating the local
//   product a_west * w. This is the only arithmetic cell in the array: one
//   signed DW x DW multiply plus an ACCW-bit add.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_pe
  #(parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator / partial-sum width (signed)
   (
    input		  clk,
    input		  rst,	      // synchronous, active high
    input		  ld,	      // weight-load shift enable
    input		  en,	      // compute enable
    input [DW-1:0]	  w_north,    // weight in from PE above
    input [DW-1:0]	  a_west,     // activation in from west
    input [ACCW-1:0]	  psum_north, // partial sum in from above
    output [DW-1:0]	  w_south,    // stored weight out to PE below
    output reg [DW-1:0]	  a_east,     // activation out to east
    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south  // partial sum out to below
    );

   // stationary weight register; presented downward for the load shift chain
   reg [DW-1:0] w;
   assign w_south = w;

   // local product: the ONLY signed operation. Operands are interpreted as
   // signed here via $signed; the 2*DW-bit result holds the two's-complement
   // product, which is then sign-extended to the accumulator width below.
   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);

   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst) begin
         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};
         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};
         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
      end
      else begin
         if (ld)
           w <= w_north;
         if (en) begin
            a_east     <= a_west;
            psum_south <= psum_north +
                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};
         end
      end
   end

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// N x N weight-stationary systolic array (the TPU MXU core).
//
// One tpu_pe per grid element, wired as a 2D mesh (per CLAUDE.md: replicated
// hardware via generate, never a procedural unroll):
//
//   - weights load from the top: w_top[j] feeds column j; each column is a
//     vertical shift register (w_south of PE(i,j) -> w_north of PE(i+1,j)).
//   - activations flow west to east: a_left[i] enters row i; a_east of PE(i,j)
//     feeds a_west of PE(i,j+1).
//   - partial sums flow north to south and accumulate; the top row's psum_north
//     is tied to zero (fresh accumulation), and c_bot[j] is the column-j result
//     leaving the bottom row.
//
// With weight B[i][j] held at PE(i,j) and activation A[m][i] entering row i,
// column j computes C[m][j] = sum_i A[m][i] * B[i][j]. Input/output skew (which
// time-aligns rows and columns) lives in the tpu top, not here.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_array
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N PEs)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	// synchronous, active high
    input		ld,	// weight-load shift enable
    input		en,	// compute enable
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_top,	// weight inputs, one per column (load)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_left,	// activation inputs, one per row
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_bot	// column results, one per column
    );

   // mesh nets: a_h carries activations east, psum_v carries sums south,
   // w_v carries weights south during load.
   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];
   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];
   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];

   genvar	   i, j;
   generate
      // west edge: activation inputs into column 0 of each row
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west
         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];
      end
      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out

      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge

         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];

      end

      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row
         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col
            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe

               .rst        (rst),

               .en         (en),

               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),

               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),
               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),
               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));
         end
      end
   endgenerate

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU).
//
// Computes one tile product C = A * B, where A and B are N x N signed operands
// (DW bits) and C is N x N signed (ACCW bits). B is the "weight" matrix, loaded
// into and held by the array; A is the "activation" matrix, streamed through; C
// drains out the bottom. Built from a tpu_array of N x N tpu_pe cells.
//
// Operation has two phases the host drives via valids:
//
//   1. Weight load: assert w_valid for N cycles. w_data carries one weight row
//      per cycle, N signed weights packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }. Because the
//      array loads top-down (shift south), push the BOTTOM matrix row first:
//      cycle k carries B[N-1-k][*]. After N cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
//
//   2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle, a_data
//      packed { col N-1 ... col 0 } = A[m][N-1..0]. Row m of the result, C[m][*]
//      (packed the same way), appears on c_data with c_valid high, 2*N-1 cycles
//      after its A row was accepted.
//
// Time alignment: row i of A is delayed i cycles entering the array (input
// skew) so partial sums chain correctly down each column; column j of the
// result is delayed N-1-j cycles (output de-skew) so a full result row leaves
// together. Both skew networks and the valid pipeline advance only with the
// array (compute enable), so the 2*N-1 latency is exact. Per CLAUDE.md the skew
// chains are generated per-row/column shift registers, not a procedural unroll.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // result / accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	 // synchronous, active high
    input		w_valid, // weight-row valid (load phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_data,	 // weight row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    input		a_valid, // activation-row valid (compute phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_data,	 // activation row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    output		c_valid, // result-row valid
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_data	 // result row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    );

   // total injection-to-output latency, in compute cycles
   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;

   // load weights while w_valid; otherwise the array computes (and drains)
   wire	      ld = w_valid;
   wire	      en = ~w_valid;

   //##########################################################################
   // Input skew: row i of the activation is delayed i cycles. Zero is injected
   // when a_valid is low so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;
   genvar	   i;
   generate
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew
         wire [DW-1:0] din =
		       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};
         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;
         end
         else begin : g_skewn
            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  sr[0] <= din;
                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // The systolic array.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;
   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array
     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),
      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));

   //##########################################################################
   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j cycles so all columns of a
   // result row line up. Column N-1 (longest path) needs no delay.
   //##########################################################################
   genvar j;
   generate
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew
         localparam D = N - 1 - j;
         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];
         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;
         end
         else begin : g_deskewn
            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  dr[0] <= cin;
                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed LAT compute cycles.
   //##########################################################################
   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;
   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst)
        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};
      else if (en)
        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};
   end
   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];

endmodule

// Self-checking testbench for the tpu benchmark (8x8 weight-stationary
// systolic matmul tile). Reads vectors.dat, one line per clock cycle:
//   w_valid w_data(64b) a_valid a_data(64b)   c_valid c_data(256b)   (hex)
// The expected outputs are the ORIGINAL logikbench tpu's per-cycle behavior;
// c_valid and c_data are compared CYCLE-ACCURATELY every cycle (c_data only
// while c_valid is high — the original holds stale data between results).
module tb;
  localparam N = 8, DW = 8, ACCW = 32;
  int total_checks;
  int total_errors;

  logic clk, rst;
  logic w_valid, a_valid, c_valid;
  logic [N*DW-1:0] w_data, a_data;
  logic [N*ACCW-1:0] c_data;

  tpu dut
    (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .w_valid(w_valid), .w_data(w_data),
     .a_valid(a_valid), .a_data(a_data), .c_valid(c_valid), .c_data(c_data));

  initial clk = 0;
  always #5 clk = ~clk;

  integer fd, rc, line_num;
  string line_buf;
  logic s_wv, s_av, e_cv;
  logic [63:0] s_wd, s_ad;
  logic [255:0] e_cd;

  initial begin
    total_checks = 0;
    total_errors = 0;
    w_valid = 0; a_valid = 0; w_data = 0; a_data = 0;
    rst = 1;
    repeat (4) @(negedge clk);
    rst = 0;

    fd = $fopen("vectors.dat", "r");
    if (fd == 0) begin
      $display("ERROR: cannot open vectors.dat");
      $fatal(1);
    end
    line_num = 0;
    while (!$feof(fd)) begin
      line_num = line_num + 1;
      void'($fgets(line_buf, fd));
      if (line_buf.len() == 0) continue;
      if (line_buf.substr(0, 0) == "#") continue;
      rc = $sscanf(line_buf, "%h %h %h %h %h %h", s_wv, s_wd, s_av, s_ad, e_cv, e_cd);
      if (rc != 6) continue;
      w_valid = s_wv; w_data = s_wd; a_valid = s_av; a_data = s_ad;
      @(negedge clk);
      total_checks = total_checks + 1;
      if (c_valid !== e_cv || (e_cv && c_data !== e_cd)) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR line=%0d exp(cv=%b cd=%h) got(cv=%b cd=%h)",
                 line_num, e_cv, e_cd, c_valid, c_data);
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
        if (total_errors > 20) begin
          $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
          $fatal(1, "FAIL (too many errors, aborting)");
        end
      end
    end
    $fclose(fd);

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
    if (total_errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

[ASSISTANT]
Good - baseline passes with cost 4,624,408. The critical path goes through the multiplier and accumulator chain. The achieved delay is 1004ps (target was 500ps). Let me think about optimization strategies:

1. **Simplify PE**: The `$signed(a_west) * $signed(w)` creates a full 16-bit signed multiplier. Since we sign-extend to 32 bits, we could potentially optimize.
2. **Accumulation tree**: The partial sum chain is a ripple through 8 PEs in a column - this creates a long combinational path (multiply + add chain through the column).
3. **Reduce area**: Remove unnecessary logic, simplify skew/deskew.

Let me first try a higher target_delay to see how area changes, and also try a lower target delay:

[ASSISTANT]
Higher target delay gives slightly less area but higher delay, so worse cost. The default 500ps target is better. Let me now try to optimize the RTL structure itself. 

Key insight: The critical path is through the multiplier → adder chain in a column (psum flows south through 8 PEs combinationally, each with a multiply+add). Let me think about the architecture. The critical path is:
- `w` (weight register) → multiply (`$signed(a_west) * $signed(w)`) → sign extend → add (`psum_north + prod_ext`) → `psum_south` register

The multiply is the expensive part. An 8x8 signed multiply produces 16 bits. But we only need it sign-extended to 32 bits for accumulation.

**Key optimization idea**: Instead of computing the full 16-bit product, I can decompose the multiply into partial products and accumulate them separately. Or better: since the accumulator is 32 bits and the product is only 16 bits, the addition `psum_north + sign_extended_product` is really just adding a 16-bit value to a 32-bit accumulator.

  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 1: try explicit partial product decomposition for the multiply\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Compute signed product using unsigned multiply + correction\n   // a_west * w where both are signed 8-bit\n   // unsigned product: a_u * w_u (16 bits)\n   // correction: if a < 0, subtract w<<8; if w < 0, subtract a<<8\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] uprod = a_west * w;  // unsigned 8x8 -> 16 bits\n   wire a_neg = a_west[DW-1];\n   wire w_neg = w[DW-1];\n   \n   // signed product = uprod - (a_neg ? w_sign_ext : 0) - (w_neg ? a_sign_ext : 0)\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_se;\n   assign prod_se = {{(ACCW-2*DW){uprod[2*DW-1]}}, uprod}\n                    - {{(ACCW-DW){a_neg}}, a_west}   // if a<0, subtract w*256\n                    - {{(ACCW-DW){w_neg}}, w};        // if w<0, subtract a*256\n   // Wait, this is wrong. [ASSISTANT]
Wait, I left the multiply as `$signed` in design_v1. Let me actually try the unsigned multiply + correction approach properly. The correct Baugh-Wooley-style correction:

For signed A * signed W (both N-bit two's complement):
- A_u * W_u (unsigned multiply, 2N bits)  
- Correction = (A_sign ? (1 << N) : 0) + (W_sign ? (1 << N) : 0)
- Signed product = A_u * W_u - (A_sign << N) * W_u... 

Actually, the simplest correct approach: `signed_prod = $signed(a) * $signed(w)`. But I want Yosys to potentially synthesize this differently. Let me try the unsigned approach properly:

If A and W are signed, A_u and W_u are their unsigned bit patterns:
- If A >= 0: A_u = A; if A < 0: A_u = A + 2^N
- signed_prod = A * W = (A_u - A_sign * 2^N) * (W_u - W_sign * 2^N)
  = A_u * W_u - A_sign * W_u * 2^N - W_sign * A_u * 2^N + A_sign * W_sign * 2^(2N)

Since A_sign and W_sign are single bits, this becomes:
- `uprod = A_u * W_u` (16-bit unsigned)
- `correction = (A_sign ? (W_u << 8) : 0) + (W_sign ? (A_u << 8) : 0) - (A_sign & W_sign ? 1<<16 : 0)`
- `signed_prod = uprod - correction`

This might actually be worse. Let me instead focus on a more impactful optimization. The real bottleneck is the 8x8 multiply + 32-bit add in a single cycle. 

Let me try a **pipelined multiply** approach: register the product, then add in the next cycle. But that changes the latency... Let me think differently.

Actually, looking at the timing path more carefully: the path starts at `w` register (the weight), goes through the multiply, then the add, to `psum_south` register. The weight `w` is a register output, and `a_west` comes from another PE's register. So both multiply inputs are registered. The critical path is: registered w → multiply → extend → add → register.

One approach: **split the multiply into two 4x8 partial products** and use a tree adder. Or use **Booth encoding**. But Yosys should already optimize the multiply.

Let me try a more structural approach - precompute partial products explicitly:

[ASSISTANT]
That's worse (4768107 vs 4624408). The 32x32 multiply is bigger. The original `$signed(a_west) * $signed(w)` with 16-bit result was better because Yosys knows it only needs 16 bits of product. Let me go back to the baseline approach and try a different optimization.

Let me think about what else could help:
1. **Deskew optimization**: The deskew registers use 32-bit registers. Total deskew stages = 0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7 = 28 stages of 32-bit registers = 896 flip-flops. The skew uses 0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7 = 28 stages of 8-bit = 224 FFs. This is significant area.
2. **Valid pipeline**: 15-bit shift register = 15 FFs.

The main area is in the 64 PEs, each with: 8-bit weight register, 8-bit activation register, 32-bit psum register = 48 FFs per PE × 64 = 3072 FFs.

Let me try to reduce the psum register width. Since the product is at most 16 bits (±128*±128 = ±16384), and we accumulate 8 of them, the maximum accumulator value is ±131072, which needs 18 bits. But intermediate partial sums could be... wait, the psum flows through the column accumulating products. At each PE, the accumulated value can grow. The worst case is the bottom PE which has accumulated 8 products. Max value = 8 * 128 * 128 = 131072 = 2^17. So we need 18 bits (17+sign).

But the output must be 32 bits. So the deskew registers must be 32 bits. But the psum registers in the PEs could potentially be narrower. However, the intermediate partial sums only go up to 8*128*128 = 131072. So we could use 18-bit accumulators in the PEs and sign-extend at the bottom. But this would change the behavior if intermediate values exceed 18 bits... Actually, 8 products of ±128*±128 = ±16384 each, so the running sum is at most 8*16384 = 131072 = 2^17. An 18-bit signed number covers ±131071. So 131072 doesn't fit in 18-bit signed (max is 131071). We need 19 bits. Let me be safe and use 20 bits.

But wait - the psum also passes through. If a partial sum is, say, 5*16384 = 81920, and we add 16384, we get 98304. All fine within 19 bits. Let me try using a narrower accumulator in the PE:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 3: narrower accumulator in PE (reduces flip-flop area)\n// Product is 16-bit max. 8 products accumulated = 19 bits needed.\n// Use 20-bit accumulator in PE, sign-extend to 32 at bottom.\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 20)  // narrow accumulator\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north +\n                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 20)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam PACCW = 20;  // PE accumulator width\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Array uses narrow accumulator internally\n   wire [N*PACCW-1:0] c_bot_narrow;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(PACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot_narrow));\n\n   // Sign-extend narrow results to full 32-bit output width\n   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_extend\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = \n            {{(ACCW-PACCW){c_bot_narrow[(j+1)*PACCW-1]}}, \n             c_bot_narrow[j*PACCW +: PACCW]};\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v3.v')

[ASSISTANT]
18 bits is too narrow - overflow! The expected result is 2 (0x00000002) but we got 0xFFFE0000 (sign-extended -2). This means the accumulator overflowed. Let me check: the expected value is 2, meaning a product like 2*8=16 is small. The problem is that 0xFFFE0000 suggests the 18-bit accumulator overflowed for a value that should be 2.

Wait, looking at the error: `got=cv=1 cd=fffe0000...` - each column is `0xFFFE0000` = -131072 (in 32-bit). But expected is `0x00000002`. So the 18-bit accumulator is returning -131072 instead of 2. This means we have -131072 in 18 bits which is `0x3FFFE` = 262142... no.

Actually -131072 in 18-bit two's complement: 131072 = 2^17, so -131072 = 0x20000 in 18 bits (0x3FFFE in 18 bits? No, 2^17 = 131072, 18 bits range is -131072 to 131071). So -131072 = 0x20000 (18 bits) = the most negative 18-bit number. When sign-extended to 32 bits: `0xFFFE0000`. So the accumulated value is -131072, but should be 2. The accumulator is overflowing because the intermediate sums exceed 18-bit range.

So 18 bits isn't enough for intermediate partial sums. The issue is that intermediate sums can be larger than the final result. For example, if you have positive and negative products, the running sum could be much larger before canceling out. With 8 products of ±16384, the running sum after k products could be up to k*16384, and with 8 products the max running sum is 8*16384=131072. But 131072 needs 18 bits (-131072 to 131071). If the running sum hits exactly 131072, that's the overflow boundary! 

Let me use 19 bits instead:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 5: 19-bit accumulator (safe for intermediate sums)\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north +\n                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
800ps target is worse (higher delay). The default 500ps seems to give the best result. Let me now try to optimize the multiply. The critical path is dominated by the 8x8 multiplier. 

Let me try decomposing the signed multiply into partial products using a Baugh-Wooley approach - this uses unsigned partial products with sign correction, which might produce a more compact/faster implementation:

Actually, let me try a different structural approach: split the 8-bit multiply into two 4x8 partial products and use a tree adder. This way, instead of one big 8x8 multiplier, we have two 4x8 multiplies and a single addition:

`a[7:0] * w[7:0] = a[3:0] * w[7:0] + (a[7:4] * w[7:0]) << 4`

This breaks the multiplier into two smaller multiplies. But Yosys might already do this internally. Let me try a different approach - use the unsigned multiply and correction:

`signed(a) * signed(w) = unsigned(a) * unsigned(w) - (a[7] ? w<<8 : 0) - (w[7] ? a<<8 : 0) + (a[7]&w[7] ? 1<<16 : 0)`

Wait, the standard formula is: if A and W are 8-bit signed, A_u and W_u are unsigned:
- A = A_u - a_sign * 256
- W = W_u - w_sign * 256
- A*W = A_u*W_u - a_sign*256*W_u - w_sign*256*A_u + a_sign*w_sign*65536

Let me try this:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 7: Baugh-Wooley style signed multiply decomposition\n// signed_prod = unsigned_prod - (a_neg << 8 * w_unsigned) - (w_neg << 8 * a_unsigned) + (a_neg & w_neg) << 16\n// Then add to accumulator\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Unsigned 8x8 multiply (16-bit result)\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] uprod = a_west * w;\n   \n   // Sign correction for two's complement multiply\n   wire a_neg = a_west[DW-1];\n   wire w_neg = w[DW-1];\n   \n   // Correction terms (all in 16-bit unsigned, then sign-extend to ACCW):\n   // if a_neg: subtract w << DW  (i.e., w_u * 256)\n   // if w_neg: subtract a << DW  (i.e., a_u * 256)\n   // if both: add 1 << (2*DW)    (i.e., +65536)\n   // But we need to be careful: w << 8 means w shifted by 8 bits\n   // If a_neg, A = A_u - 256, so A*W_u = A_u*W_u - 256*W_u\n   // But W_u is the unsigned value of w, which when a is negative means...\n   // Actually the correct formula for sign-extended result:\n   // signed_prod = uprod - (a_neg ? (w << DW) : 0) - (w_neg ? (a_west << DW) : 0) + (a_neg & w_neg ? (1 << (2*DW)) : 0)\n   // But this doesn't account for w and a being unsigned here...\n   \n   // Let me think again. A = a_west (8 bits, could be negative)\n   // W = w (8 bits, could be negative)\n   // A_u = a_west (unsigned interpretation), W_u = w (unsigned interpretation)\n   // A = A_u - a_neg * 2^8 (as a signed number)\n   // W = W_u - w_neg * 2^8\n   // A*W = A_u*W_u - a_neg*2^8*W_u - w_neg*2^8*A_u + a_neg*w_neg*2^16\n   // = uprod - (a_neg ? (W_u << 8) : 0) - (w_neg ? (A_u << 8) : 0) + (a_neg & w_neg ? (1 << 16) : 0)\n   \n   // But W_u << 8 in 16 bits = {w, 8'b0} which is a 16-bit value\n   // For the correction, we need: corr = (a_neg ? {w, 8'b0} : 0) + (w_neg ? {a_west, 8'b0} : 0) - (a_neg & w_neg ? 16'h10000 : 0)\n   // Wait, +1<<16 in 16 bits is 0... so in 17+ bits: bit 16 is set\n   \n   // The signed product as 17-bit value:\n   // prod_17 = uprod[15:0] - (a_neg ? {w, 8'b0} : 16'b0) - (w_neg ? {a_west, 8'b0} : 16'b0) + (a_neg & w_neg ? 17'h10000 : 17'b0)\n   \n   // Actually let's just sign-extend uprod and the corrections to ACCW and compute\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] uprod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){uprod[2*DW-1]}}, uprod};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_a = a_neg ? {{(ACCW-DW){1'b0}}, w, {DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_w = w_neg ? {{(ACCW-DW){1'b0}}, a_west, {DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_both = (a_neg & w_neg) ? {{(ACCW-2*DW-1){1'b0}}, 1'b1, {2*DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod = uprod_ext - corr_a - corr_w + corr_both;\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north + prod;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam PACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*PACCW-1:0] c_bot_narrow;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(PACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot_narrow));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [PACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot_narrow[j*PACCW +: PACCW];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = \n               {{(ACCW-PACCW){cin[PACCW-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [PACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; [ASSISTANT]
The Baugh-Wooley correction is wrong. Looking at the errors: expected `000028de` got `ffff28de`. The values are off by the correction term. The issue is in the sign extension of the corrections. Let me re-examine:

The correction terms `corr_a` and `corr_w` use `{{(ACCW-DW){1'b0}}, w, {DW{1'b0}}}` which puts w in the upper bits. But `w` is the unsigned 8-bit value, and `{w, 8'b0}` = w * 256 = w << 8. The issue is that the sign extension of `uprod` is wrong - `uprod` is an unsigned product, but I'm sign-extending it with `uprod[15]`. For the Baugh-Wooley method, the final signed product doesn't need sign extension of `uprod` because the corrections handle the sign.

Actually the real issue: `uprod_ext = {{(ACCW-16){uprod[15]}}, uprod}` sign-extends the unsigned product using bit 15. But uprod is unsigned, so bit 15 is just the MSB, not a sign bit. The corrections are supposed to convert the unsigned product to signed. The `uprod` should be zero-extended, not sign-extended. Let me fix:
  -> edit_file(filename='design_v7.v', old_string="   wire [ACCW-1:0] uprod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){uprod[2*DW-1]}}, uprod};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_a = a_neg ? {{(ACCW-DW){1'b0}}, w, {DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_w = w_neg ? {{(ACCW-DW){1'b0}}, a_west, {DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_both = (a_neg & w_neg) ? {{(ACCW-2*DW-1){1'b0}}, 1'b1, {2*DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};", new_string="   wire [ACCW-1:0] uprod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){1'b0}}, uprod};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_a = a_neg ? {{(ACCW-2*DW){1'b0}}, w, {DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_w = w_neg ? {{(ACCW-2*DW){1'b0}}, a_west, {DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_both = (a_neg & w_neg) ? {{(ACCW-2*DW-1){1'b0}}, 1'b1, {2*DW{1'b0}}} : {ACCW{1'b0}};")

[ASSISTANT]
The Baugh-Wooley approach is much worse (3,947,385 vs 3,213,387) - the extra correction logic adds area and delay. Yosys's built-in `$signed` multiply is better optimized. Let me abandon that approach and go back to design_v6.

Now let me think about what else could help. The critical path is: `w` register → 8x8 signed multiply → sign-extend → 19-bit add → `psum_south` register. 

What if I try to reduce the adder depth? Currently it's `psum_north + sign_extended_prod`. The prod is 16 bits, psum_north is 19 bits. The add is a 19-bit add. Since the lower 16 bits of the product contribute to the lower 16 bits of the sum, and only bits 16-18 need carry from the product, maybe I can simplify.

Actually, let me try another approach: **pipeline the multiply**. Register the product, then add in the next cycle. This would split the critical path but change latency. But changing latency breaks the cycle-accurate contract...

Wait - what if I use the fact that the multiply result goes through `a_east` as well? No, `a_east` just passes the activation through.

Let me try a different idea: **combine the multiply and add into a single fused multiply-accumulate (MAC) expression** which Yosys might optimize differently:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 8: try fused MAC and also try explicit carry-save in PE\n// Based on v6 (19-bit narrow accumulator, narrow deskew)\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Try: compute the product and sum directly as a single expression\n   // psum_south = psum_north + sign_extend(a_west * w)\n   // The signed multiply produces 16 bits, sign-extend to 19 bits, add to 19-bit psum\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   \n   // Try direct add without intermediate\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            // fused multiply-accumulate\n            psum_south <= $signed(psum_north) + $signed(prod_ext);\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam PACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*PACCW-1:0] c_bot_narrow;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(PACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot_narrow));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [PACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot_narrow[j*PACCW +: PACCW];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = \n               {{(ACCW-PACCW){cin[PACCW-1]}}, cin};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [PACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {PACCW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            wire [PACCW-1:0] dout = dr[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = \n               {{(ACCW-PACCW){dout[PACCW-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v8.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Interesting - the `$signed` version has lower delay (767 vs 813) but higher area (4282 vs 3949), resulting in worse cost (3,287,579 vs 3,213,387). The v6 design (unsigned multiply with explicit sign extension) is still better.

Let me now try combining the best of both: v6's approach but with explicit $signed on the add to potentially help the synthesizer. Actually wait - let me look more carefully. v6 uses `psum_north + {{ext}, prod}` (unsigned add), v8 uses `$signed(psum_north) + $signed(prod_ext)` (signed add). The signed version gave less delay but more area. 

Let me try a completely different approach to reduce area: **remove the `a_east` register when possible**. The activation flows west-to-east through registered hops. But actually, I can't remove it - it's part of the systolic timing.

Let me instead try to **reduce the deskew depth**. The deskew uses N-1-j stages for column j, totaling 0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7=28 stages of 19-bit registers = 532 FFs. That's significant. Can I somehow combine the deskew with the sign extension?

Actually, let me think about another approach: what if I fold the sign extension INTO the deskew? The deskew stores 19-bit values, and at the output I sign-extend. That's already what v6 does. 

Let me try reducing the deskew further by noting that the deskew doesn't need the sign bits. Since the values fit in 19 bits, the deskew only needs the lower 19 bits... which it already uses.

Let me try a different angle: **remove the reset on skew/deskew registers**. If the testbench only checks after reset, the initial values don't matter. Actually, the spec says "rst asserted only before the stream begins" and is async active-high. Let me check if removing resets helps area:

[ASSISTANT]
Removing reset from vpipe broke things - the `c_valid` is high from the start because the uninitialized `vpipe` register has X or random values. The vpipe needs reset because `c_valid` is checked every cycle. Let me restore the reset on vpipe but keep the skew/deskew resets removed. Actually, the skew/deskew registers also feed into the computation chain... if they're uninitialized, the psum values could be garbage during the initial cycles.

Actually wait - the PEs still have reset. The skew and deskew just shift zeros during `en` cycles. During reset, `en` is low (since w_valid might be high during loading). Let me think... During reset, `en = ~w_valid`. If w_valid is 0, en=1, and the skew/deskew would shift. If w_valid is 1, en=0, and they hold. But rst is async and asserted before the stream begins. When rst is deasserted, the PEs are reset to 0. But the skew/deskew are uninitialized.

The issue is that vpipe was uninitialized and happened to have X values, making c_valid=X which doesn't match expected 0. Let me keep reset on vpipe but try removing it from skew/deskew only. Actually, looking at the errors, the vpipe issue causes c_valid=1 when it should be 0. Let me restore vpipe reset and also keep skew/deskew resets:

i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam PACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
Same result - the delay is already at the minimum Yosys can achieve. Let me now try to optimize the PE multiply + add path more aggressively. 

The critical path is through the 8x8 signed multiply. Let me try decomposing the multiply using **partial products explicitly** - split a[7:0] into a[3:0] and a[7:4], and compute two partial products. The key insight: a[7:4] * w is only 4x8, and when shifted by 4, it only affects the upper bits. This means the lower 4 bits of the product come only from a[3:0]*w, and the carry from that to the upper bits:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 11: split multiply into two 4x8 partial products\n// a * w = (a_lo + a_hi * 16) * w = a_lo * w + (a_hi * w) << 4\n// where a_lo = a[3:0], a_hi = a[7:4] (both unsigned for the multiply,\n// then correct for sign)\n// Actually, let's keep $signed but let Yosys decide. Instead try:\n// Pre-register the weight sign extension and just do unsigned multiply\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Split a into low 4 and high 4 bits\n   wire [3:0] a_lo = a_west[3:0];\n   wire [3:0] a_hi = a_west[7:4];\n   \n   // Partial products: a_lo * w (unsigned 4x8 = 12 bits) and a_hi * w (4x8 = 12 bits)\n   wire [11:0] pp_lo = a_lo * w;       // a_lo * w (unsigned)\n   wire [11:0] pp_hi = a_hi * w;       // a_hi * w (unsigned)\n   \n   // Combine: full unsigned product = pp_lo + (pp_hi << 4) (16 bits)\n   wire [15:0] uprod = {4'b0, pp_lo} + {pp_hi, 4'b0};\n   \n   // Sign correction for signed multiply\n   wire a_neg = a_west[7];\n   wire w_neg = w[7];\n   // signed_prod = uprod - (a_neg ? (w << 8) : 0) - (w_neg ? (a_west << 8) : 0) + (a_neg & w_neg ? (1 << 16) : 0)\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] uprod_ext = {{(ACCW-16){1'b0}}, uprod};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_a = a_neg ? {{(ACCW-8){1'b0}}, w, 8'b0} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_w = w_neg ? {{(ACCW-8){1'b0}}, a_west, 8'b0} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] corr_both = (a_neg & w_neg) ? {{(ACCW-17){1'b0}}, 1'b1, 16'b0} : {ACCW{1'b0}};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod = uprod_ext - corr_a - corr_w + corr_both;\n\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north + prod;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam PACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; [ASSISTANT]
Much worse (4,375,492). The split multiply with correction logic is terrible. Yosys's built-in signed multiply is much better. Let me go back to design_v10 and try other approaches.

Let me now try to **reduce the deskew area** more aggressively. The deskew stores 19-bit values. Total deskew FFs = 0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7 = 28 stages × 19 bits = 532 FFs. 

What if I move the deskew to BEFORE the sign extension, and use a smaller width? The psum values from the bottom of the array are 19 bits. But I could potentially store only the lower 16 bits (the product part) and the sign... no, the accumulated sum needs all 19 bits.

Let me try a different approach: **combine the accumulator and deskew into fewer stages**. Actually, the deskew is necessary for column alignment. Let me instead try to reduce the **skew** registers. The skew uses 0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7 = 28 stages × 8 bits = 224 FFs. This is relatively small.

Let me try yet another approach - can I use a **smaller accumulator** by using a different accumulation order? What if instead of accumulating in the psum chain, I accumulate the products separately and add them at the end? But that changes the architecture fundamentally...

Actually, let me try to **reduce the PE's psum register** by using carry-save representation. Instead of a 19-bit ripple carry accumulator, use carry-save (sum + carry vectors). This would reduce the adder delay but increase register count. Let me try:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 12: carry-save accumulation in PE\n// Instead of psum_south = psum_north + prod (one big adder),\n// use carry-save: store sum and carry separately, combine at the bottom.\n// This reduces the critical path per PE from (multiply + add) to (multiply + CSA)\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_n,    // sum in\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  pcarry_n,  // carry in\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_s,    // sum out\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_s   // carry out\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   \n   // Carry-save: psum_s = psum_n ^ prod_ext ^ pcarry_n\n   //             pcarry_s = (psum_n & prod_ext) | (psum_n & pcarry_n) | (prod_ext & pcarry_n)\n   // This is a 3:2 compressor (full adder)\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] a = psum_n;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] b = prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] c = pcarry_n;\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs = a ^ b ^ c;                           // sum\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cc = (a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c);          // carry (unshifted, carry to next stage)\n   // The carry should be shifted left by 1 in a proper carry-save, but since\n   // we accumulate the carry into the next PE, we need to shift it.\n   // Actually in a proper CSA accumulator chain, the carry from stage k \n   // feeds into stage k+1 shifted by 1. But the psum chain is spatial (PE to PE),\n   // not temporal. So we can't easily do carry-save across PEs.\n   // \n   // Let me instead use a simpler approach: just use the original add but\n   // try a different accumulator width.\n\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_s     <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         pcarry_s   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_s     <= cs;\n            pcarry_s   <= {cc[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};  // shift carry left\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\n// This approach has a fundamental problem: the carry from one PE needs to \n// be added in the next PE, but the carry-save representation needs the final\n// adder at the end. Also the carry shift means the carry from PE(i,j) goes to\n// PE(i+1,j) but shifted by 1 bit, which means PE(i+1,j) needs to handle an\n// extra carry input. This doesn't work cleanly in a systolic array.\n// \n// Let me abandon this and go back to the simpler approach.\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign pcarry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j] + pcarry_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
Excellent! Carry-save accumulation is a big win! Cost dropped to 3,069,081 (from 3,177,840). The delay dropped to 696ps (from 803ps) because the CSA (3:2 compressor) is much faster than a full adder, and the critical path is now through the CSA instead of the full ripple-carry add. The area went up (4407 vs 3956) but the delay reduction more than compensates.

But wait - there's a correctness concern. The carry-save approach should be functionally correct since we add the carry at the bottom. Let me verify it passes (it does - 0/6782 errors). 

Now let me try to optimize further. The area increased because we now have two 19-bit registers (sum + carry) per PE. Can I reduce the carry width? The carry is shifted left by 1, so the LSB is always 0 and the MSB falls off. Also, for the carry-save chain, the carry only needs to be as wide as needed.

Actually, looking more carefully: the product is 16 bits (sign-extended to 19). The carry from a CSA of two 19-bit numbers is at most 19 bits (but shifted left by 1, so bit 0 is always 0 and bit 18 is lost). So the carry really only needs bits [18:1] = 18 bits. And the sign extension... let me think about whether the carry needs sign extension.

Actually, the carry-save approach has a subtlety: the carry chain isn't a proper carry-propagate chain. Let me verify the approach is sound by checking if there might be overflow issues with larger test vectors. It passed the full testbench, so it seems fine.

Let me try to **narrow the carry register** since the carry is always shifted left by 1:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 13: carry-save with narrower carry register\n// Carry is shifted left by 1, so LSB is always 0 and MSB is dropped\n// Only need bits [18:1] = 18 bits for [ASSISTANT]
The narrow carry doesn't work because the carry-save chain needs the full-width carry. When I drop the MSB of the carry, I lose information. The carry-save approach requires full-width carry propagation through the chain. The issue is that the carry from PE(i) feeds into PE(i+1), and the carry needs to be properly aligned.

Looking at the error: expected `0002` but got `0040` - the values are off by a factor of 32 (shifted). The carry shifting is wrong. In a proper CSA accumulator, the carry from stage k is shifted left by 1 and added to stage k+1. But the spatial arrangement means the carry from PE(i,j) goes to PE(i+1,j), and it needs to be shifted left by 1. This means PE(i+1,j)'s carry input is `{carry_in[17:0], 1'b0}` which is the carry from PE(i,j) shifted left.

The problem is that the carry is shifted each hop, so after 8 hops, it's shifted by 8 bits. This means the carry-save chain only works correctly if the carry propagation is properly handled. The v12 design used full 19-bit carry (no truncation) and it worked. Let me go back to v12 and instead try to optimize differently.

Actually wait - v12 used full-width carry and it worked. The issue in v13 is truncating the carry to 18 bits and losing the MSB. Let me go back to v12's approach but try to reduce area in other ways.

Actually, the carry from a CSA of three 19-bit values: the carry is at most 19 bits (before shift). After shift, bit 0 is 0 and bit 19 is lost (or needs to be bit 20). But since the values are bounded, the carry won't actually use all bits. The issue is that I need to preserve the full carry width.

Let me try a different optimization: **reduce the sum and carry width**. The product is 16 bits. The running sum after k PEs is at most k*16384 + k*16384 (carry) ≈ 2k*16384. After 8 PEs: 2*8*16384 = 262144 = 2^18. So I still need 19 bits for the sum. But the carry is bounded similarly...

Actually, CSA doesn't work that way - sum and carry are unsigned bits, not signed values. The combined value is sum + carry. Both can be up to the full range. So I need both to be wide enough to hold the final result.
