i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [PACCW-1:0] sum_in = c_sum[j*PACCW +: PACCW];\n         wire [PACCW-1:0] carry_in = c_carry[j*PACCW +: PACCW];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            // No deskew, just do the final add\n            wire [PACCW-1:0] result = sum_in + {carry_in[PACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = \n               {{(ACCW-PACCW){result[PACCW-1]}}, result};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [PACCW-1:0] sum_r [0:D-1];\n            reg [PACCW-1:0] carry_r [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1) begin\n                    sum_r[k] <= {PACCW{1'b0}};\n                    carry_r[k] <= {PACCW{1'b0}};\n                  end\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sum_r[0] <= sum_in;\n                  carry_r[0] <= carry_in;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1) begin\n                    sum_r[k] <= sum_r[k-1];\n                    carry_r[k] <= carry_r[k-1];\n                  end\n               end\n            end\n            wire [PACCW-1:0] result = sum_r[D-1] + {carry_r[D-1][PACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = \n               {{(ACCW-PACCW){result[PACCW-1]}}, result};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_v14.v')

550 gives the same as 500 (same synthesis result). The 600ps target is the sweet spot so far. Let me now try a different structural optimization. 

Looking at the v12 critical path again: the path goes through the multiplier (XOR/XNOR chain for partial products) then the CSA. The multiply is 8x8 signed and is the main bottleneck. Let me try **unsigned multiply + sign correction done at the CSA level** - since the CSA adds three values, I could incorporate the sign correction as a third input to the CSA:

Actually, let me try a completely different approach. What if I use **Booth encoding** for the multiplier? Booth encoding reduces the number of partial products from 8 to 4, potentially reducing the multiply delay. But Yosys should already optimize this...

Let me instead try to **reduce the CSA width**. The product is 16 bits. The carry chain shifts left each stage, so after stage k, the carry is shifted by k bits. The lower bits of the carry become "settled" and don't need to be stored. But this optimization is complex and might not help.

Let me try a simpler optimization: **reduce the CSA width to 17 bits** (the meaningful product width) and handle the upper sign bits separately. The product is ±16384, which is 15 bits + sign = 16 bits. After 8 accumulations, max = ±131072 = 17 bits + sign = 18 bits. In CSA, the sum and carry each need to be 19 bits... but what if I use 17-bit CSA and handle the sign extension outside?

Actually, let me try a completely different approach: **pre-register the product**. If I split the PE into two stages (product register → CSA register), the critical path is halved but the latency increases by 1. But that breaks cycle accuracy...

Wait - what if I restructure the PE so the multiply feeds into a product register, and the CSA uses the registered product? The latency would change though. Let me think about how to compensate...

Actually, the systolic array has 8 PEs in a column. The psum flows through 8 registered stages. If I add one more register stage, the latency increases by 1 cycle per column, which changes the total latency from 2N-1 to 2N. That breaks the spec.

Let me try another approach: **merge the sign extension into the CSA**. Instead of sign-extending the 16-bit product to 19 bits and then CSA, let me directly handle the sign at the CSA level. The product's sign bit (bit 15) should be replicated for bits 16-18. In the CSA, this means:
- For bits 0-15: normal CSA
- For bits 16-18: the product input is the sign bit, and we need to handle sign extension

This is essentially what we're already doing. Let me try to reduce the width by noting that for bits 16-18, the sum and carry don't need full CSA - they just need to propagate the sign.

Let me try a different optimization: **use a half-adder for the LSB of the CSA** since the carry-in to bit 0 is always 0 (shifted carry). And for the MSB, the carry-out doesn't need to be stored (it would shift off the top):

Actually, let me focus on what's achievable. The v12 design at 600ps target gives 3,027,016. Let me try to make the design file be v12 and name it design.sv, and try a few more optimizations.

Let me try **reducing the carry width** properly. In v12, the carry is stored as 19 bits but shifted left by 1, so bit 0 is always 0 and bit 18 (the MSB of the original) is lost. So I only need to store bits [17:0] of the carry (18 bits), since bit 0 is always 0 (implied). When reading, I reconstruct as {carry[17:0], 1'b0}:

Wait, that's what v13 tried and it failed. Let me re-examine why v13 failed. The carry output `cc` is 19 bits. I stored `cc[18:1]` (18 bits). When reading, I did `{pcarry_n, 1'b0}` = `{cc_prev[18:1], 1'b0}` which gives `cc_prev[18:1] << 1`. But the actual carry should be `cc_prev << 1` = `{cc_prev[18:0], 1'b0}` which is 20 bits! I'm losing bit 0 of cc_prev!

The issue: when I store `cc[18:1]` and reconstruct as `{cc[18:1], 1'b0}`, I get `{cc_prev[18:1], 0}`. But the correct reconstruction should be `{cc_prev[18:0], 0}` (the full 19-bit carry shifted left by 1 = 20 bits, but we only need the lower 19 bits). So `cc_prev << 1` = `{cc_prev[17:0], 1'b0}` (19 bits, losing the MSB). 

Ah, so the correct narrow carry is: store `cc[17:0]` (18 bits), reconstruct as `{cc_stored[17:0], 1'b0}` (19 bits). The MSB of cc (bit 18) is lost because the shift would push it to bit 19 which overflows. But that means we lose information!

The carry at bit 18 matters because the accumulated sum can reach 131072 = 2^17, which is bit 17. The carry into bit 18 (the sign bit) matters. So I can't drop it.

Actually, wait. Let me reconsider. The carry `cc` is a 19-bit value. When shifted left by 1, we get a 20-bit value, but we only keep 19 bits (bits [18:0] of the shifted value). So the original bit 18 of cc is lost (it would go to bit 19). But the original bit 18 of cc represents a carry out of the MSB, which in two's complement arithmetic is the carry-out that doesn't affect the sign-extended result. In signed arithmetic, the carry-out of the MSB doesn't matter (it's the sign extension).

So the correct approach is: store `cc[17:0]` (18 bits), reconstruct as `{cc_stored[17:0], 1'b0}` (19 bits). The bit 18 of cc (carry out of sign bit) is correctly dropped.

Let me verify this is what v13 tried... In v13, I stored `cc[ACCW-1:1]` = `cc[18:1]` (18 bits) and reconstructed as `{pcarry_n, 1'b0}` = `{cc_prev[18:1], 1'b0}` (19 bits). But that's wrong! I should store `cc[17:0]` (18 bits) and reconstruct as `{cc_stored[17:0], 1'b0}`.

The difference: v13 stored bits [18:1] and lost bit 0. But bit 0 of cc (carry from the LSB) matters! It gets shifted to bit 1. So the correct approach is to store bits [17:0] (losing bit 18, the sign carry-out) and reconstruct by shifting left.
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 15: CSA with properly narrowed carry (18 bits, lose MSB only)\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19,\n    parameter CW = 18)  // carry width = ACCW-1\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_n,\n    input [CW-1:0]\t  pcarry_n,   // carry in (bits [16:0]... no, [17:0])\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_s,\n    output reg [CW-1:0]   pcarry_s\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   \n   // 3:2 compressor (CSA)\n   // carry input: pcarry_n is bits [17:0] of the carry, shifted left by 1\n   // reconstruct: {pcarry_n, 1'b0} = carry shifted left (19 bits)\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] carry_in = {pcarry_n, 1'b0};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] a = psum_n;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] b = prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] c = carry_in;\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs = a ^ b ^ c;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cc = (a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c);\n   // carry_out: store only bits [17:0] (bit 18 = sign carry-out, can be dropped)\n   wire [CW-1:0] cc_stored = cc[CW-1:0];\n\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_s     <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         pcarry_s   <= {CW{1'b0}};\n      end\n   assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign pcarry_v[0][j] = {CW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         // Final carry-propagate add at the bottom\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j] + {pcarry_v[N][j], 1'b0};\n      end\n      for              .pcarry_s   (pcarry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {PACCW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + Hmm, the narrow carry version (v15) is worse than v12 (3,114,504 vs 3,027,016). The area saving from 1 bit per carry register (8×8=64 FFs) isn't worth the slightly worse delay. Let me go back to v12.

Let me try another approach: **reduce the sum width in the CSA**. The product is 16 bits (sign-extended to 19). But the sum after k products needs at most 17+k bits... Actually, the CSA sum needs to be full width because the carry feeds back.

Let me try a different optimization direction. What if I **remove the `a_east` register** and instead use combinational pass-through for the activation? The activation flows west-to-east through registered hops. If I make it combinational (just a wire), the latency changes. But the systolic timing requires the registered hops...

[ASSISTANT]
Sync reset is slightly worse (3,105,844 vs 3,027,016 for async). The async reset is better. Let me go back to v12 and try different things.

Let me try to **reduce the sum width to 17 bits** while keeping the CSA. The product is 16 bits. With 8 products, max is ±131072 = 2^17. In CSA, the sum and carry each carry the full 18-bit range (including sign). But 17 bits (without sign) can hold 0 to 131071. The sign needs an extra bit. So 18 bits unsigned = 19 bits signed? No...

Actually, the CSA operates on unsigned bit vectors. The sum and carry are unsigned representations of parts of a signed number. The sign is handled by sign-extending the product. So both sum and carry need to be 19 bits (to hold ±131072 = 2^17 which needs 18 bits unsigned or 19 bits signed).

Hmm, but actually: 131072 = 2^17. In unsigned, that needs 18 bits (0 to 262143). In signed two's complement, ±131072 needs 18 bits (-131072 to 131071... no, -131072 to 131071 is 18 bits signed but -131072 is the boundary). So 19 bits signed covers ±131072. That's what we're using.

Let me try to reduce to 18 bits and see if it passes:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 17: v12 with 18-bit accumulator (trying if CSA avoids overflow)\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 18)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_n,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  pcarry_n,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_s,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_s\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] a = psum_n;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] b = prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] c = {pcarry_n[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs = a ^ b ^ c;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cc = (a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c);\n\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_s     <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         pcarry_s   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_s     <= cs;\n            pcarry_s   <= cc;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 18)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
18 bits still overflows (same issue as before - the intermediate sums can reach ±131072 which needs 19 bits). So 19 is the minimum for the CSA approach too. Let me stick with v12.

Let me now try a different approach: instead of CSA, try to use a **pipelined adder** approach. What if I split the accumulation across two cycles by using a different PE arrangement? No, that changes latency.

Let me try to **reduce the deskew area** by using a **shared** approach. The deskew delays column j by N-1-j cycles. What if I instead delay ALL columns by N-1 cycles (the maximum), using N-1 stages for all? That would be 8 × 7 × 19 = 1064 FFs instead of 532. Worse!

What about the opposite: **remove the deskew entirely** and output results as they arrive? No, the spec requires all columns of a row to arrive together.

Let me try to **merge the final carry-propagate adder** into the deskew. Currently, the final add `psum + carry` is combinational at the bottom of each column. What if I register the sum and carry separately through the deskew and do the final add at the output? That was v14 which was worse. 

Let me instead try to **reduce the multiply delay** by using a different representation. What if I use **unsigned multiply** and handle sign outside the critical path? The signed multiply might be slower than unsigned:

Let me try: `wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = a_west * w;` (unsigned) and then sign-correct using the CSA's third input. The CSA adds psum + prod + carry. If I make prod unsigned and add the sign correction as a separate value...

Actually, let me try the simplest approach: just change `$signed(a_west) * $signed(w)` to unsigned multiply and see if Yosys gives a faster multiplier:

[ASSISTANT]
Much worse - the sign correction logic adds significant delay and area. Yosys's built-in `$signed` multiply is much better. Let me abandon this and go back to v12.

Let me now try to optimize the **deskew** by reducing its width. What if the deskew uses fewer bits? The final result fits in 19 bits, but what if I only delay the lower 16 bits and compute the upper 3 bits differently? No, that doesn't make sense.

Let me try a different approach: **fold the final adder into the deskew** by using a registered adder. In v12, the bottom of each column has `psum + {carry_shifted}` which is a combinational 19-bit add. What if I register this add and reduce the deskew by 1 stage? The deskew for column j is D = N-1-j. If I use a registered final add, I need D-1 deskew stages + 1 registered add stage = D stages total. Same total, but the registered add might be faster (the 19-bit add is off the critical path).

Wait, the 19-bit add is already at the bottom and feeds into the deskew. The critical path doesn't go through the deskew (it goes through the multiply+CSA). So optimizing the deskew won't help delay. But it could help area.

Let me try to use **fewer deskew registers** by noting that the deskew only needs to delay the result, not compute. What if I use a **single shared delay line** for all columns? No, each column needs a different delay.

Let me try to **reduce area by using a smaller valid pipeline**. The vpipe is 15 bits. What if I use a counter instead? A counter would need fewer bits (4 bits to count to 15) but the comparison logic might not be smaller. Actually, a shift register of 15 bits is 15 FFs. A 4-bit counter + comparator is 4 FFs + some logic. But the behavior is different - the counter needs to track compute cycles, which is complex with the stall semantics.

Let me try another approach: can I **remove the weight shift register** (`w_south`)? The weight is loaded top-down and shifts south. After loading, the weight is stationary. What if I load weights directly into each PE using a broadcast instead of a shift chain? No, the spec says weights shift south.

Actually, looking at the weight load: the PE captures `w_north` on `ld`, and `w_south = w` (combinational). So during load, the weight shifts down through the column. After loading, the PE holds its weight. This is a simple shift register. The `w_south` wire is used only during loading.

Let me try to reduce the `a_east` register. The activation flows west-to-east through registered hops. Each PE registers the activation. But what if I use a **single shared activation register per row** instead of per-PE? No, the systolic timing requires one register hop per PE.

Let me now try to explore the design space more. Let me try **merging the CSA carry into the sum** by using a different accumulation strategy. What if instead of CSA, I use a **4:2 compressor** that combines the product and the incoming sum/carry more efficiently?

Actually, a 4:2 compressor is essentially two CSAs in series. That won't help here since we only have 3 inputs.

Let me try one more thing: **remove the carry register entirely** and use a different approach. What if I compute `psum_s = psum_n + prod_ext` (the original ripple add) but **only for the lower bits**, and use CSA for the upper bits? This is a hybrid approach:

Actually, let me try something simpler: use a **carry-lookahead adder** for the 19-bit add. Let me write the adder explicitly using the prefix adder structure from the reference:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 19: CSA approach (v12) with explicit carry-select adder at bottom\n// Try to reduce the bottom adder delay\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_n,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  pcarry_n,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_s,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_s\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] a = psum_n;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] b = prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] c = {pcarry_n[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs = a ^ b ^ c;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cc = (a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c);\n\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_s     <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         pcarry_s   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_s     <= cs;\n            pcarry_s   <= cc;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
Worse than v12. The `$signed` on the add didn't help. Let me go back to v12.

Now let me try to think about what's really driving the area. The v12 design has:
- 64 PEs × (8-bit w + 8-bit a_east + 19-bit psum + 19-bit pcarry) = 64 × 54 = 3456 FFs
- Skew: 28 × 8 = 224 FFs
- Deskew: 28 × 19 = 532 FFs
- vpipe: 15 FFs
- Total: ~4227 FFs

The area is 4396 (at 600ps target). The CSA adds logic per PE (XOR + majority for 19 bits). The multiply adds a lot of logic (8x8 signed multiply).

Let me try to **reduce the CSA width** by noting that the product is only 16 bits. For bits 0-15, we need the full CSA. For bits 16-18 (sign extension), we don't need the CSA - we just need to propagate the sign. Let me try using a 16-bit CSA and handling the upper 3 bits with simple sign-extension logic:
  -> create_file(content="// Optimized version 20: CSA only on lower 16 bits, sign-extend upper\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19,\n    parameter PW = 16)  // product width\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_n,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  pcarry_n,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_s,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_s\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   \n   // CSA on lower PW bits only\n   wire [PW-1:0] a_lo = psum_n[PW-1:0];\n   wire [PW-1:0] b_lo = prod[PW-1:0];\n   wire [PW-1:0] c_lo = pcarry_n[PW-1:0];  // carry LSB is always 0 (shifted)\n   \n   wire [PW-1:0] cs_lo = a_lo ^ b_lo ^ c_lo;\n   wire [PW-1:0] cc_lo = (a_lo & b_lo) | (a_lo & c_lo) | (b_lo & c_lo);\n   \n   // Upper bits (16-18): sign extension\n   // The product sign is prod[15]. The psum and carry upper bits are sign extensions.\n   // For the upper bits, we need: sum_upper = psum_upper + sign_extend(prod) + carry_upper\n   // But since these are sign extensions, the arithmetic is simpler.\n   // Actually, for proper CSA, we need to handle the carry from bit 15 to bit 16.\n   // The carry out of the lower CSA (cc_lo[15]) feeds into the upper part.\n   wire cc_out = cc_lo[PW-1];  // carry out from bit 15\n   \n   // Upper bits: psum[18:16] + prod_sign_ext + pcarry[18:16] + cc_out\n   // prod_sign_ext = {3{prod[15]}}\n   // This is a 4-input addition for 3 bits. We can do this with a small ripple adder.\n   wire [2:0] up_lo = psum_n[PW+2:PW];      // bits 16-18 of sum\n   wire [2:0] up_c  = pcarry_n[PW+2:PW];     // bits 16-18 of carry  \n   wire [2:0] up_p  = {3{prod[PW-1]}};       // sign extension of product\n   wire [3:0] up_sum = {1'b0, up_lo} + {1'b0, up_c} + {1'b0, up_p} + cc_out;\n   // Wait, up_c is the carry, which was shifted. So the carry for bit 16 is\n   // the carry out of bit 15 of the previous stage's CSA, which is cc_out.\n   // But pcarry_n is the stored carry from the previous PE, which includes\n   // bits [18:0] (shifted left by 1). So pcarry_n[16] is the carry for bit 16\n   // from the previous stage... Actually no. The carry is stored as the full\n   // cc from the previous PE, and when read, it's shifted left by 1.\n   // So {pcarry_n[18:0], 1'b0} is the carry input.\n   // For bit 16, the carry input is pcarry_n[15] (which was cc_lo[15] of prev stage).\n   // Wait, this is getting confusing. Let me just do the full-width CSA.\n   \n   // Actually, let me just use the full 19-bit CSA like v12.\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] a = psum_n;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] b = prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] c = {pcarry_n[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n   \n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs = a ^ b ^ c;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cc = (a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c);\n\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_s     <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         pcarry_s   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_s     <= cs;\n            pcarry_s   <= cc;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign pcarry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j] + {pcarry_v[N][j][ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_n     (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .pcarry_n   (pcarry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_s     (psum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .pcarry_s   (pcarry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam PACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*PACCW-1:0] c_bot_narrow;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(PACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot_narrow));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; [ASSISTANT]
The v10 (non-CSA) with high target_delay gives 3,383,927 - worse than v12's 3,027,016. The CSA approach is still better.

Let me now copy v12 as the main design file and try a few more things. Let me try to **remove the `a_east` register** from the CSA PE and instead pass the activation combinationally. The systolic array requires activation to be delayed by one cycle per column, but the delay is actually provided by the `a_east` register. If I remove it, the activation would reach all columns in the same cycle, which would break the timing.

Actually, wait - let me re-examine the systolic array timing. The activation `a_west` enters the PE and is registered as `a_east` for the next column. The psum also flows south with a register. So each PE adds one cycle of delay to both the activation (east) and the partial sum (south). The input skew adds 0-7 cycles of delay for rows 0-7, so the activation arrives at column 0 of row i at cycle t+i. After 8 hops (columns), the activation has been delayed by 8 more cycles, but it's the psum that matters for the output.

Let me try one more structural idea: **use a 4:2 compressor** by combining the product's sign extension into the carry. This might reduce the effective CSA width.

Actually, let me try a simpler optimization: **gate the clock** on the skew registers when not in use. No, that won't help synthesis area/delay.

Let me try to **reduce the deskew by noting that the final result is at most 18 bits** (since max is ±131072). So I only need 18 bits of deskew, not 19. Wait, I already use 19 (PACCW=19). Can I use 18? No, that overflows.

Let me try to save design_v12 as design.sv and try one final optimization - use the $signed add in the CSA:

k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {PACCW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            wire [PACCW-1:0] dout = dr[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = \n               {{(ACCW-PACCW){dout[PACCW-1]}}, dout};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design.sv')

[ASSISTANT]
Now let me try to see if I can make the CSA use the `prod` directly without sign extension, and instead incorporate the sign extension into the carry chain. The idea: instead of sign-extending `prod` to 19 bits, use the 16-bit prod directly and handle the upper 3 bits of sign extension by adding the sign bit to the carry:

Actually, let me try a different approach. What if the CSA only operates on 16 bits and the upper 3 bits are handled by a simple 3-bit adder? This reduces the CSA from 19 bits to 16 bits, saving 3 bits of XOR + majority per PE:
  -> create_file(content="// design_opt1.sv - CSA on 16 bits, 3-bit adder for upper sign extension\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19,\n    parameter PW = 16)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_n,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  pcarry_n,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_s,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_s\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   \n   // Lower 16-bit CSA\n   wire [PW-1:0] a_lo = psum_n[PW-1:0];\n   wire [PW-1:0] b_lo = prod;\n   wire [PW-1:0] c_lo = pcarry_n[PW-1:0];\n   \n   wire [PW-1:0] cs_lo = a_lo ^ b_lo ^ c_lo;\n   wire [PW-1:0] cc_lo = (a_lo & b_lo) | (a_lo & c_lo) | (b_lo & c_lo);\n   wire carry_out_lo = cc_lo[PW-1];\n   \n   // Upper 3 bits (16-18): sign extension + carry\n   // psum_n[18:16], pcarry_n[18:16], prod sign (3 bits), carry_out_lo\n   // This is a 4-input 3-bit addition\n   // But we need CSA for upper bits too (to maintain carry-save)\n   // Upper sum = psum_hi ^ prod_sign ^ carry_hi ^ carry_out_lo\n   // Upper carry = majority of those 4... but CSA only handles 3 inputs\n   // Let's split: first CSA: (psum_hi, prod_sign_ext, carry_hi)\n   // Then: second CSA: (result_sum, carry_out_lo, 0)\n   // Or just do a 4-input add for 3 bits (small)\n   wire [2:0] psum_hi = psum_n[ACCW-1:PW];\n   wire [2:0] carry_hi = pcarry_n[ACCW-1:PW];\n   wire [2:0] prod_sign = {3{prod[PW-1]}};\n   \n   // 4-bit addition (with carry_out_lo as carry in)\n   wire [3:0] upper = {1'b0, psum_hi} + {1'b0, carry_hi} + {1'b0, prod_sign} + carry_out_lo;\n   \n   assign psum_s = {upper[2:0], cs_lo};\n   // For carry: the carry out from the upper addition\n   // carry_s = upper carry-out (bit 3 of upper) shifted + cc_lo shifted\n   // Actually in CSA, carry is shifted left. So:\n   // carry_s[18:1] = {upper_carry_out, upper[2:0]}... this is getting complex.\n   // Let me just compute the full carry properly.\n   // The carry from the lower CSA: cc_lo (shifted left by 1)\n   // The carry from the upper addition: upper[3] (the carry out)\n   // Combined carry (shifted left by 1):\n   // pcarry_s = {upper[3], upper[2:0], cc_lo[14:0], 1'b0}... no\n   // \n   // Actually, the CSA carry should be:\n   // For bits 0-14: cc_lo[i] (shifted to i+1)\n   // For bit 15: carry_out_lo (shifted to 16)\n   // For bits 16-18: depends on upper addition carries\n   //\n   // This is a mixed CSA/ripple approach. The carry propagation between\n   // the lower CSA and upper adder creates a ripple path, but only 3 bits.\n   \n   // Let me compute the carry properly:\n   // carry_s[0] = 0 (always, since shifted)\n   // carry_s[1] = cc_lo[0]\n   // carry_s[2] = cc_lo[1]\n   // ...\n   // carry_s[15] = cc_lo[14]\n   // carry_s[16] = carry_out_lo (= cc_lo[15])\n   // carry_s[17] = upper carries... \n   // This doesn't work cleanly because the upper is a ripple adder,\n   // not a CSA. The carry from the upper addition needs to be stored\n   // and propagated, but it's not a simple shift.\n   \n   // Let me just use the full 19-bit CSA (same as v12).\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] a = psum_n;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] b = prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] c = {pcarry_n[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs = a ^ b ^ c;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cc = (a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c);\n\n   always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_s     <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         pcarry_s   <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_s     <= cs;\n            pcarry_s   <= cc;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] pcarry_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign pcarry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j] + {pcarry_v[N][j][ACCW-2:0], 1'b0};\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_n     (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .pcarry_n   (pcarry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_s     (psum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .pcarry_s   (pcarry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam PACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk or posedge rst) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*PACCW-1:0] c_bot_narrow;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(PACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot_narrow));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; ## Summary

### What Worked Best

The best design is **`design_v12.v`** (also saved as `design.sv`), achieving cost **3,027,016** (down from the baseline 4,624,409 — a 35% improvement). Key optimizations, in order of impact:

1. **Carry-Save Accumulation (CSA) in PE** — biggest single win (3,177,840 → 3,027,016). Replaced the 19-bit ripple-carry add `psum_north + prod_ext` with a 3:2 compressor (`cs = a ^ b ^ c`, `cc = (a&b)|(a&c)|(b&c)`), with the carry shifted left and propagated to the next PE. Final carry-propagate add only at the column bottom. This cut critical-path delay from ~803ps to ~688ps because CSA logic is much shallower than a ripple adder.

2. **Narrow Accumulator (32→19 bits)** — second biggest win (4,624,409 → 3,213,387 for non-CSA, 3,177,840 with async reset). Products are ±16384 (16-bit), and 8 of them sum to ±131072, needing only 19 bits signed. Narrowed both the PE psum registers and deskew shift registers, then sign-extended to 32 bits at the output. Also tried 18-bit (overflowed) and 20-bit (slightly worse).

3. **Async Reset** — small but consistent improvement (3,213,387 → 3,177,840 for the non-CSA version). Changed `posedge clk` to `posedge clk or posedge rst`, which mapped to different (smaller) flip-flop types.

4. **Target delay sweep** — 600ps gave the best cost for the CSA design (3,027,016 vs 3,069,081 at 500ps).

### What Didn't Work / Caused Regressions

- **Baugh-Wooley unsigned multiply + sign correction** — much worse (3,947,385). The extra correction logic added area and delay; Yosys's built-in `$signed(a*w)` multiplier is better optimized.
- **Split multiply (two 4×8 partial products)** — much worse (4,375,492). Similar reasons.
- **32-bit sign-extended multiply** — worse area (4,769 vs 4,624 baseline) because Yosys couldn't exploit the narrow product.
- **Narrow carry register (18 bits)** — correctness failure or slight regression. Dropping carry MSB lost information; the area saving (64 FFs) didn't offset the delay impact.
- **Moving final add past deskew (separate sum/carry deskew)** — worse (3,310,103). Extra deskew registers for the carry vector cost more than they saved.
- **`$signed()` annotations on CSA operations** — slightly worse; the synthesizer already handled unsigned bit operations optimally.
- **Synchronous reset** — slightly worse than async (3,105,844 vs 3,027,016).

### Lessons Learned

1. **Carry-save accumulation is the key lever for systolic MAC chains** — the per-PE ripple adder was the critical-path bottleneck, and replacing it with a 3:2 compressor (depth-1 XOR + majority gate per bit) dramatically cut delay at a modest area cost.
2. **Narrowing datapaths to their true required width is extremely effective** — going from 32 to 19 bits saved area in both flip-flops and combinational logic, and reduced adder depth.
3. **Let Yosys handle signed multiply** — manual decomposition (Booth, Baugh-Wooley, split partial products) consistently produced worse results than the native `$signed(a)*$signed(w)`.
4. **Target delay is a minor knob** — the RTL structure (CSA vs ripple, accumulator width) was the real lever, with target_delay providing ~3% secondary tuning.
5. **Always verify correctness after width changes** — 18-bit accumulator passed simulation briefly but overflowed on edge cases with large intermediate partial sums.
